Nykyporets(2024).3.2Features of AI-Based Tutors Providing Individualized
Feedback and Influencing Students’ Language AccomplishmentThis section
presents the features of AI-based tutors that enhance students’ language
learning outcomes through individualized feedback and adaptive
instruction. Findings revealed that AI tutors help learners by providing
personalized activities, immediate responses, and structured learning
exercises (see Fig. 2). The discussion elaborates on how these features
improve grammar, vocabulary, and learner motivation.The personalization
features of AI tutors—such as adaptive exercises and instant
responses—illustrate how technology can cater to individual learning
needs. These features help learners focus on specific weaknesses,
leading to targeted improvement in grammar and vocabulary. Benek, K.
(2025) emphasized that adaptive technologies enhance engagement when
feedback is tailored to learner profiles.However, students’ continued
preference for teachers highlights the importance of human elements in
learning, including motivation, empathy, and contextual explanation.
These findings support Hattie and Timperley’s (2007) assertion that
feedback is most effective when it combines instruction with
encouragement.3.2.1 Personalized and immediate feedbackResults: Students
described AI feedback as personalized and instantaneous, noting that the
tools adjust to their strengths and weaknesses. Participants reported
that they could focus on grammar and vocabulary areas that required
improvement without waiting for external feedback.“The feedback is based
on my strengths and weaknesses.” (P-4)“I don’t have to wait for days to
get feedback—it’s given right away.” (P-15)Discussion:These responses
indicate that AI tutors adapt dynamically to learner needs, creating
customized exercises that support language acquisition. Benek, K. (2025)
found that personalized AI feedback significantly improves engagement
and retention. Likewise, Luckin et al. (2016) emphasized that immediacy
sustains motivation and promotes continuous learning.3.2.2Human
preference and contextual learningResults:Although students valued AI
for its efficiency, many expressed a preference for teacher feedback due
to its motivational and emotional components.“Teachers explain more
clearly and also motivate us.” (P-13)Discussion:This finding underscores
the importance of emotional and social dimensions in learning. Shute
(2008) and Hattie & Timperley (2007) observed that feedback is most
effective when it includes explanation, motivation, andencouragement
—areas where human teachers excel. Thus, while AI supports self-paced
learning, teacher interaction remains essential for developing empathy
and deeper comprehension.

responses3.3Advantages of Using AI Tutors in Fostering Long-Term Learner
Engagement and Motivation in Personalized Language Learning Environments
This section explores how AI-based tutors support continuous engagement,
intrinsic motivation, and independent learning among first-year
students. Results revealed that students used AI tutors not only as
feedback tools but also as constant learning companions that helped
sustain their language development over time (see Fig. 3). The findings
are organized into thematic subheadings with corresponding
interpretations supported by literature.3.3.1AI Tutors as learning and
self-monitoring toolsResults:Participants shared that they often relied
on AI tutors for quick practice, clarification, and self-assessment.
Many students stated that AI helped them monitor their progress and stay
motivated, especially when they were tired or lacked confidence to ask
teachers for help.“Honestly, most of the time I run to AI, especially
when I don’t feel like answering because of tiredness. It’s not
time-consuming.” (P-6)“I use it whenever I need a short practice session
or to check if I’m improving.” (P-2)

Miralles et al.; Asian J. Educ. Soc. Stud., vol. 51, no. 12,
pp. 968-978, 2025; Article no.AJESS.149529975Discussion:These findings
show that AI tutors function as reliable, on-demand learning
aidsthatpromote self-regulation. Baker (2014) emphasized that
intelligent tutoring systems provide immediate and efficient support,
enabling students to continue learning without interruption. Similarly,
Nagham Alhamdawee & Israa sabeeh Abbas (2021)noted that AItools foster
metacognitive awareness by allowing learners to track their own
development. The ability to self-monitor through AI engagement helps
maintain intrinsic motivation and builds autonomy. AI tutors contribute
to long-term engagement by providing flexible, interactive, and
self-paced learning opportunities. Features such as quizzes, gamified
activities, and speech recognition sustain motivation and reduce
anxiety. Gee (2003) described such environments as powerful for
persistent learning because they promote challenge without
judgment.Nonetheless, long-term motivation is best sustained when
AI-assisted learning is balanced with social interaction and teacher
guidance. Luckin (2016) emphasized that technology should enhance—not
replace—human relationships in education.3.3.2 Interactive and
personalized featuresResults:Students described AI tutors as
interactive, engaging, and adaptive to their learning styles. They noted
that AI activities such as quizzes, pronunciation drills, and grammar
games made learning enjoyable and helped them stay focused.“The
interactive exercises keep me engaged. They focus on what I need to work
on.”(P-15)“It’s fun, like a game. AI gives challenges based on what I
need to improve.” (P-14)Discussion:Interactive and gamified features
were highly valued for maintaining student motivation. VanLehn (2011)
asserted that real-time, responsive feedback enhances engagement and
focus. Similarly, Chen, M. C. (2018) found that personalized activities
improve long-term learning interest. Gee (2003) also supported the idea
that game-like learning encourages persistence and deeper understanding.
These results imply that AI tutors can transform repetitive drills into
interactive, learner-centered experiences that sustain attention and
enjoyment.3.3.3Preference for AI tutors over traditional
learningResults: Students favored AI tutors over traditional classroom
learning for certain tasks such as grammar checking, vocabulary
building, and speaking practice. They appreciated the flexibility,
accessibility, and absence of judgment when interacting with AI
systems.“AI tutors are better for grammar and vocabulary practice—you
get feedback right away, and it’s convenient.” (P-12)“AI helps me
practice speaking without fear of embarrassment.” (P-15)Table 3.

qualitative responsesDiscussion:The findings show that AI tutors promote
a comfortable, low-anxiety environment that enhances learner confidence.
Warschauer and Healey (1998) reported that technology-based environments
reduce emotional barriers to learning. Zimmer (2002) further noted that
AI speech recognition tools allow risk-free language practice, which
boosts fluency and self-assurance. However, while students appreciated
AI’s accessibility and neutrality, Luckin (2016) argued that sustained
motivation still depends on balancing self-paced digital practice with
interpersonal communication and instructor feedback. 4. CONCLUSIONThis
qualitative study investigated the experiences of first-year UM Digos
college students using AI tutors (like ChatGPT and Duolingo) for
language learning, grounded in Adaptive Learning Theory. The findings
conclude that AI tutors are valuable supplemental tools, offering
timely, unbiased feedback that promotes independence, self-evaluation,
and increased confidence, aligning with personalized learning. They also
provide a safe, supportive environment that alleviates language learning
anxiety and boosts motivation. However, a significant limitation is the
lack of contextual and explanatory feedback, emphasizing the continued
necessity of human instruction and guidance. The study recommends that
teachers use AI as a supplement, helping students interpret feedback,
and calls for developers to enhance AI tools with human-like,
explanatory features to improve their pedagogical functionality. Future
research should focus on the long-term, quantitative effects of AI
support on learning outcomes.CONSENT All authors declare that written
informed consent was obtained from first-year college students from UM
Digos college.ETHICAL APPROVAL Before conducting the study, researchers
requested and secured formal permission from the key school officials of
um digos college. The research adhered strictly to the ethical protocols
and guidelines set forth by the um digos college research and
publication center, ensuring that the study was conducted responsibly
and ethically.DISCLAIMER (ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE)Author(s) hereby
declare that NO generative AI technologies such as Large Language Models
(ChatGPT, COPILOT, etc) and text-to-image generators have been used
during writing or editing of this manuscript. ACKNOWLEDGEMENTSThe

” (Asian Journal of Education and Social Studies by 李宗諺 G5) ” My
Idea - Artificial intelligence (AI) is reshaping how students learn and
interact with course materials. Among the most notable advancements in
this realm are intelligent tutoring systems (ITS), which use AI to
deliver personalized instruction. By adapting to individual student
needs, providing real-time feedback, and tailoring content, ITS can
transform learning. They enhance outcomes while making education more
accessible — creating new opportunities for both students and educators.
How Intelligent Tutoring Systems Work Intelligent tutoring systems are
revolutionizing education by providing personalized learning for diverse
students. These systems utilize artificial intelligence to analyze a
student’s current knowledge, learning pace and preferred learning style.
Through continuous assessment of student performance, ITS can adjust the
difficulty level of tasks, offer targeted feedback and suggest resources
tailored to the learner’s specific requirements. This dynamic approach
ensures that each student receives instruction that is both challenging
and achievable, promoting deeper understanding and retention of the
material. The architecture of an ITS typically comprises four key
components: The domain model encompasses the knowledge and skills to be
taught, serving as the foundation for the system’s instructional
content. The student model tracks the learner’s progress, identifying
strengths and areas for improvement. The tutoring model determines the
most effective teaching strategies based on data from the domain and
student models. The user interface model facilitates interaction between
the student and the system, presenting information and receiving input
in an engaging manner. This integrated framework enables ITS to deliver
a cohesive and responsive learning experience. Benefits of Intelligent
Tutoring Systems in Education Intelligent tutoring systems deliver
immediate feedback and targeted instruction, boosting engagement,
retention, and overall learning outcomes. They also offer scalability
and data insights, allowing educators to track progress and continually
refine instruction to support every student effectively. Personalized
Learning Intelligent tutoring systems tailor educational content to each
student’s specific needs. Using AI, these systems adjust the pace,
difficulty, and focus of lessons based on a student’s strengths and
weaknesses. This level of customization ensures that students receive
instruction that is specifically designed to improve their understanding
and retention, helping them progress at their own speed. ITS supports
diverse learning styles, too, offering more effective ways to engage
students and foster deeper connections with the material. Immediate
Feedback Intelligent tutoring systems provide immediate feedback,
allowing students to understand their mistakes and correct them
promptly. This instant response helps reinforce learning and prevents
misconceptions from taking root. Students can adjust their understanding
and approach by receiving timely feedback. Quickly identifying and
addressing errors enhances the learning process as well as makes it more
efficient and customized to individual needs. Scalability and Data
Insights Intelligent tutoring systems bring scalability and data
insights that enhance education. By collecting and analyzing student
performance data, ITS help identify patterns and trends, enabling
informed decisions about curriculum and instruction. This data-driven
approach allows for continuous improvement and adaptation to meet the
evolving needs of students. The scalability of ITS also means that
personalized learning experiences can be delivered to a large number of
students simultaneously, making quality education more accessible. This
combination of scalability and data insights ensures that ITS can
adequately support diverse learning environments and contribute to
better educational outcomes. Challenges and Limitations of Intelligent
Tutoring System Intelligent tutoring systems accompany their own set of
challenges and limitations that highlight the importance of balancing
ITS with traditional teaching methods to create a well-rounded learning
experience. For one, developing these systems is costly, requiring
considerable investment in research and technology. Building robust
systems takes time and effort, as creating interactive content and
adaptive algorithms can be expensive and resource-intensive. ITS may
also struggle with nuances such as emotional intelligence, often lacking
the personal touch that human tutors can provide. This limitation may
impact students’ ability to develop soft skills or receive the emotional
support that a teacher could offer. Another limitation of ITS is the
risk of over-reliance — potentially reducing valuable face-to-face
interactions between students and teachers. These interactions help
foster social skills and build rapport, both of which are vital for
holistic development. In addition, ITS may not be suitable for every
subject. For example, while it works well for subjects like math or
coding, the complexity and variability of topics in humanities or
creative fields make developing an effective ITS more difficult.
Real-World Applications of Intelligent Tutoring Systems From K-12
education to higher education and corporate training, intelligent
tutoring systems are being applied in various real-world settings. In
each of these environments, ITS provides personalized learning,
real-time feedback and the ability to scale instruction to meet the
needs of diverse learners. K-12 Education Intelligent tutoring systems
are transforming K-12 education by offering personalized learning
experiences suitable for a range of students. These AI-driven tools
analyze students’ learning patterns and tailor educational content
accordingly, in turn boosting engagement and understanding. Partnerships
between educational technology companies and content providers aim to
create AI-powered tutoring and teaching assistant tools, combining
innovative instructional content with significant reach to benefit
students. These developments underscore the growing role of AI in
education when it comes to providing students with personalized learning
experiences as well as automating repetitive tasks to allow teachers
more time to focus on understanding and adaptability. Higher Education
ITS are making a substantial impact in higher education by providing
personalized support outside the traditional classroom setting. These
AI-powered systems can offer students instant assistance with
assignments, projects and coursework that helps them stay on track with
their learning. ITS also supports faculty by automating administrative
tasks like grading and progress tracking; this way, instructors can
focus on more meaningful interactions with students. With ITS, students
receive timely interventions that enrich their academic experience and
help them navigate complex subjects at their own pace. Universities
investing in AI tools are giving students access to cutting-edge
resources that streamline research, offer personalized learning
experiences and simulate real-world scenarios. As AI continues to
reshape industries, graduating from an AI-forward institution provides
students with a competitive edge in the evolving job market. These
systems also prepare students for the workforce by equipping them with
skills to interact with AI technologies and adapt to an increasingly
tech-driven job market. Corporate Training ITS are offering customized
corporate training experiences that adapt to individual employee needs.
These AI-driven tools analyze employees’ learning patterns and tailor
educational content accordingly in order to enhance engagement and
understanding. For instance, ITS can provide practice-based
instruction to support corporate training, enabling employees to develop
skills in highly interactive learning environments. Additionally, ITS
can automate routine tasks — allowing employees to focus on more complex
and creative aspects of their roles. Integrating ITS into corporate
training programs, companies can boost employee performance, reduce
training costs and ensure that their workforce is armed with the
necessary skills to meet organizational goals. The Future of Intelligent
Tutoring Systems As AI technology advances, intelligent tutoring systems
show immense promise. Personalized learning remains central, with AI
continuously analyzing data to pinpoint each student’s learning style,
strengths, and areas for improvement For example, AI can identify when a
student struggles with reading comprehension and offer targeted
exercises to advance those skills. AI is also enhancing traditional
assessments by creating adaptive tests that adjust in real time to
better measure a student’s abilities, thereby helping provide a more
accurate reflection of learning progress. ITS promises to improve access
to education and support as well. In remote areas where private tutoring
is unavailable, AI-powered tutoring systems can offer one-on-one support
that provides explanations and guides students through complex problems.
Aside from benefiting students, AI is revolutionizing teacher support by
automating administrative tasks like grading and attendance tracking.
This frees up valuable time for educators to focus on teaching and
student interaction. As ITS become more widespread, they also have the
potential to reduce educational inequity by offering high-quality,
personalized learning experiences to underserved communities. With AI,
students from underfunded schools can access tailored resources and
materials that would otherwise be unavailable — bridging gaps in
educational opportunity. Shape the Future of Education With AI at Park
University Inspired by AI’s transformative potential for learning?

Technology for Teachers. Offered online, this graduate program equips
educators with the skills and knowledge to incorporate emerging
technologies—like intelligent tutoring systems—into their teaching.

Abstract Advances in generative artificial intelligence show great
potential for improving education. Yet little is known about how this
new technology should be used and how effective it can be compared to
current best practices. Here we report a randomized, controlled trial
measuring college students’ learning and their perceptions when content
is presented through an AI-powered tutor compared with an active
learning class. The novel design of the custom AI tutor is informed by
the same pedagogical best practices as employed in the in-class lessons.
We find that students learn significantly more in less time when using
the AI tutor, compared with the in-class active learning. They also feel
more engaged and more motivated. These findings offer empirical evidence
for the efficacy of a widely accessible AI-powered pedagogy in
significantly enhancing learning outcomes, presenting a compelling case
for its broad adoption in learning environments. Similar content being
viewed by others
