Information -  The Crisis of Idea Homogenization in AI Collaboration
Summary-
https://theconversation.com/uni-students-are-using-ai-to-ask-stupid-questions-and-get-feedback-on-their-work-263535
As artificial intelligence becomes common in education, students often
rely too much on it during creative problem-solving. This leads to
limited innovation and similar ideas. Based on distributed cognition
theory, researchers explore how strengthening human agency can improve
collaborative creative outputs and support learner development. ”
(Breaking Overreliance by 王一安 G7) ” New Information - From
Independent Thinking to Deep Integration Summary -
https://theconversation.com/uni-students-are-using-ai-to-ask-stupid-questions-and-get-feedback-on-their-work-263535
Results showed that only asking students to think independently before
collaborating with AI improved initial prompt quality and perceived
cognitive growth. However, because human agency did not last, it did not
actually help the final creative output. When the experiment required
students to integrate their own ideas with AI-generated content, human
agency and prompt quality improved consistently. This successfully
enhanced the quality of the final creative output and perceived
cognitive growth. ” (Finding Solutions by 王一安 G7) ” New Information
- Human-Led Idea Integration is Key to Innovation Summary -
https://theconversation.com/uni-students-are-using-ai-to-ask-stupid-questions-and-get-feedback-on-their-work-263535
The findings extend the application of distributed cognition theory to
AI-supported creativity. They clearly point out that human-led idea
integration plays a critical and irreplaceable role in the collaboration
process. This discovery provides a solid, evidence-based perspective for
designing future computer-supported learning environments and
cultivating innovation skills. ” (Research Conclusion by 王一安 G7) ” My
Idea -Practicing Problem-Solving Skills- Since the tool can generate

risk is blind acceptance, meaning information literacy must be

ultimately points toward ensuring students are equipped to handle
external environmental changes.

” (PBL Emhancement by 王一安 G7) ” New Information - Information
Processing vs. Genuine Cognition -
https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0360131525002015 The
literature indicates that AI’s strong performance is primarily driven by
non-creative mechanisms, such as searching, integrating existing
information, and probabilistic reasoning. This fundamentally differs
from the flexible and diverse thinking patterns of humans. In an
educational context, this defines AI as an assistive tool. When
practicing problem-solving, students must retain their cognitive
agency—using AI’s vast database for retrieval and reasoning rather than
outsourcing the entire thought process. ” (Defining the Boundaries by
王一安 G7) ” New Information - Stimulating Potential with Novel Ideas -

transform problem-solving from a passive search for a single standard
answer into a dynamic process of receiving external inspiration,
verifying information, and discovering optimal solution paths. ”

Personalized Scaffolding (個人化知識拆解)
【小標：動態情境輸出與批判性評估策略 (Dynamic Contextual Outputs and
Evaluative Strategies)】 論文原文抓取： “Unlike earlier AI systems that
operated as static knowledge repositories, generative AI’s dynamic,
context-sensitive outputs require learners to develop new regulatory
strategies for critical evaluation and appropriate reliance.” 對應分析：
這段內文強調生成式 AI
能提供「動態且具備情境敏感性」的內容，這正是高階個人化鷹架的特徵。它不再只是單向給予知識，而是強迫學習者在互動中發展出「批判性評估」的新策略，將學習從被動接收轉化為對複雜資訊的拆解與判斷。

2.  Efficiency Optimization (學習效率最佳化)
    【小標：提示工程與自動化反饋的教務實踐 (Prompt Engineering and
    Automated Feedback Practices)】 論文原文抓取： “The keyword
    co-occurrence analysis revealed five thematic clusters: … 3) higher
    education practices (feedback, academic writing), 4) instructional
    design with LLMs (prompt engineering, automation)…” 對應分析：
    該研究透過數據分析確認了「反饋 (Feedback)」與「學術寫作 (Academic
    Writing)」是 AI 在高等教育中的核心應用群組。透過「提示工程 (Prompt
    Engineering)」與「自動化 (Automation)」，AI
    能在大學寫作過程中實現即時、大規模的反饋循環，極大地優化了學生的學習速度與學術產出效率。

3.  Self-Regulated Learning & Lifelong Skills (自主學習與終身技能)

    Scaffolds and AI Regulation Assessment)】 論文原文抓取： “The study
    identifies three strategic directions: 1) embedding metacognitive
    scaffolds in AI interfaces rather than providing direct answers…
    and 3) evolving assessment practices to evaluate learners’ AI
    regulation capabilities.” 對應分析：
    這段原文是支持「終身技能」最強大的證據。論文主張將「元認知鷹架」嵌入界面，引導學生思考而非僅給答案。更重要的是，它提出未來評量的重點應在於學生的「AI
    調節能力（AI regulation
    capabilities）」——這是一項讓學生在離開大學後，仍能自主控制 AI
    工具、驗證資訊並持續自我成長的數位時代核心終身技能。 ” (1 by 陳宥穎

• Cognitive Atrophy: Thinking is a “muscle.” If AI does the work, your
brain weakens. • No Struggle, No Learning: True education requires the
effort of research. AI skips the learning and only gives the result. •
Kills Creativity: AI uses old data to give “average” answers, destroying
originality. • Loss of Autonomy: Dependence on AI makes students unable
to think independently. ” (Is AI dulling our minds? by 趙詠恩 G7) ” New
Information - https://www.mdpi.com/2075-4698/15/1/6  - • Cognitive
Atrophy: Thinking is like a “mental muscle.” Outsourcing tasks to AI
causes our critical reasoning to weaken from disuse. • Death of Inquiry:
Critical thinking is developed through the struggle of researching and
analyzing. AI skips this process, giving answers without understanding.
• Passive Learning: Users become “passive recipients” of information.
This kills the habit of questioning sources and evaluating logic. • The
“Average” Trap: AI provides the most “probable” answer based on old
data, which restricts independent and original thought. ” (Impacts on
Cognitive Offloading and the Future of Critical Thinking by 趙詠恩 G7)
“     My Idea - The impact of the employment market -      Many
repetitive jobs or basic writing and design positions are facing the
threat of being replaced by AI.” (The impact of the employment market by
施芮安 G7) ” New Information
-  https://www.bcg.com/publications/2026/ai-will-reshape-more-jobs-than-it-replaces-
Five years later - or perhaps in the longer future - 10% to 15% of jobs
in the United States may be canceled. 1 This potential level of
unemployment is quite considerable, creating an important appeal for
business leaders. ” (Source by 施芮安 G7) ” My Idea - Limits
Creativity -      AI often generates common or predictable responses,
which may prevent students from expressing unique thoughts. As a result,
their creativity and personal writing style may not improve. ” (Limits
Creativity by 趙詠恩 G7) ” New Information -  - In the research ,data
was collected through a questionnaire survey. The results showed that
participants generally believed that uncontrolled reliance on AI would
negatively impact critical thinking, problem-solving skills, research
skills, memory, reasoning abilities, creativity, writing skills, and
comprehension of complex texts. Furthermore, respondents indicated that
excessive AI use fosters passive learning, reduces learning engagement,
weakens independent learning and trial-and-error learning abilities, and
increases the risk of academic misconduct.

” (Content by 施芮安 G7)

“     New Information - Artificial intelligence can also personalize
student learning. By analyzing student performance data, AI-powered
tools can determine which students need support to improve their
learning experience, and the best ways to help those students. -     ”
(Personalization by 唐秉村 G8) “     New Information - If a student
feels “stuck” while working on an assignment, artificial intelligence
programs can provide immediate, helpful assistance if a teacher or
caregiver isn’t available. For instance, a student can ask, “How do I
solve for X?” to be reminded of the steps for solving an equation. A
student can even ask, “What are some effective strategies for improving
my essay writing?” and AI can offer advice and resources right away. -  
  ” (Speed by 唐秉村 G8) “     New Information - AI programs can help
individualize learning opportunities for students. For instance, ChatGPT
can quickly and easily translate materials to another language, making
it easier for students who speak another language to understand
assignments. ChatGPT can also revise materials so they are suitable for
varying grade levels and tailor projects to suit students’ skills and
interests. -     ” (Individualization by 唐秉村 G8) ” New Information
- It can provide greater access to resources. Educators can access a
wealth of AI-powered platforms to facilitate and enhance the learning
that takes place in their classrooms. Just a few examples are Canva
Magic Write, which helps in brainstorming, outlining, and lesson
planning; Curipod, which enables teachers to quickly create interactive
lessons; Eduaide, which provides teachers with more than 100 resource
types to choose from to create high-quality instructional materials; and
Quizzizz, which can be used to design quizzes that will create a
personalized learning path based on each student’s responses. -

” (inclusive lessons by 郭承恩 G8) ” New Information - The lesson
happening in this classroom is remarkable for two reasons. First,
children with and without disabilities are learning side by side.
Children with disabilities are one of the most marginalized groups in
society and face multiple barriers that make it hard – or even
impossible – to attend school and learn. For most of the 240 million
children with disabilities worldwide, an inclusive classroom like the
one in Uruguay is still out of reach. To address this disparity, UNICEF
launched the Accessible Digital Textbooks (ADT) initiative in 2014.
These curriculum-aligned books are designed using Universal Design for
Learning (UDL) principles: they offer content in multiple formats, such
as audio narration or simplified text, and give students flexible ways
to learn. Textbooks are a central pillar of learning, offering a
structured and reliable foundation developed by education experts.
Making them accessible is essential for inclusive education. Today, ADTs
are being developed in six countries across Latin America and the
Caribbean, three countries in Eastern and Southern Africa and two in
South Asia. However, creating a single ADT can still take months, making
it difficult to scale and meet growing demand. This brings us to the
second reason this classroom in Uruguay is remarkable: the ADT was
created with the help of Artificial Intelligence (AI). In 2024, UNICEF
began exploring how generative AI can make the ADT development process
faster, more cost-effective, and easier to scale. By using AI
responsibly, countries can more efficiently convert standard curriculum
materials into accessible formats – while respecting the content
holders’ intellectual property and adhering to review processes led by
Ministries of Education (MoE), stakeholders, and education experts. The
goal is to accelerate the creation of accessible learning materials like
ADTs, removing barriers to educational content for all children and
making inclusive education not only possible, but sustainable. - ”
(example by 郭承恩 G8) ” New Information - It can be used to create and
supplement content. Through AI-powered platforms, you can create
lessons, activities, assessments, discussion prompts, and presentations
simply by providing a short prompt with keywords. -
from:https://education.illinois.edu/about/news-events/news/article/2024/10/24/ai-in-schools–pros-and-cons

Intelligence (AI) improves human potential by increasing efficiency,
supporting creativity, and enhancing innovation. It can process large
amounts of data in a very short time, allowing people such as
researchers and writers to focus more on creative and strategic work
instead of repetitive tasks. In addition, AI helps with brainstorming
and idea generation, which can lead to new developments in different
fields like art and medicine. However, AI is most effective when used as
a collaborative tool rather than a replacement for human thinking. With
proper human supervision (human-in-the-loop), AI can serve as a powerful
tool that accelerates progress while still keeping humans in control.

    EFFICIENCY : the use of ChatGPT improved communication efficiency

    G8) “     New Information - One of the most significant benefits of
    AI in higher education is its ability to tailor educational
    experiences to individual students. AI-powered learning platforms
    use data-driven algorithms to assess student performance and adapt
    content to meet their specific learning needs. This personalized
    learning approach reduces stress by allowing students to progress at
    their own pace and receive targeted support, improving both academic
    performance and emotional well-being. -     ” (Benefits of AI on
    students by 吳翰威 G8) ” New Information - Privacy risks have been a
    concern for as long as AI has been around. People are wary about
    what personal data is collected and how it is used—and whether they
    have any knowledge of or control over how it is used. Many voice
    concerns about how carefully their data is stored and how protected
    it is from being leaked. Other worries include having private and
    sensitive information viewed by others, having false or misleading
    information disseminated, and the increasing ease others have in
    accessing others’ personal data. Broadly speaking, risks are
    involved in data collection, data processing, data dissemination,
    and invasion (encroaching into an individual’s personal space,
    choices, or activities).

-   from:https://education.illinois.edu/about/news-events/news/article/2024/10/24/ai-in-schools–pros-and-cons
    ” (Privacy and Security Concerns by 郭承恩 G8) ” New Information -
    The idea that the Internet is making us stupid is legitimate, and AI
    is like the Internet on steroids. With AI bots doing everything from
    research to writing papers, from basic math to logic problems, from
    generating hypotheses to performing science experiments, from
    editing photos to creating “original” art, students of all ages will
    be tempted (and many will succumb to the temptation) to use AI for
    their school work, undermining education goals. “The academic
    struggle for students is what pushes them to become better writers,
    thinkers and doers. Like most positive outcomes in life, the
    important part is the journey. Soon, getting college degrees without
    AI assistance will be as foreign to the next generation as payphones
    and Blockbuster [are to the current generation], and they will
    suffer for it,” says Mark Massaro, professor of English at Florida
    SouthWestern State College. A June 2023 study found that increased
    use of AI correlates with increased student laziness because of a
    loss of human decision-making. Similarly, an October 2023 study
    found not only increased laziness and carelessness but also a
    decline in work quality when humans worked alongside AI robots. The
    implications of allowing AI to complete tasks are enormous. We will
    see declines in work quality and human motivation as well as the
    rise of dangerous situations from deadly workplace accidents to
    George Orwell’s dreaded “groupthink.” And, when humans have become
    too lazy to program the technology, we’ll see lazy AI, too

-   from:https://www.britannica.com/procon/artificial-intelligence-AI-debate

    唐秉村 G8) “     New Information - Students can use AI to write
    entire essays, answer quiz questions, or do their homework.
    Ironically, now there are AI programs that can detect AI writing to
    help teachers determine if their students are cheating. But
    sometimes those programs may falsely identify a student’s original
    work as plagiarism. -     ” (Cheating by 唐秉村 G8) ” New
    Information - The growing use of artificial intelligence in recent
    years has revolutionised the operation of some sectors. Take
    academic research as an example. From summarising dense documents to
    assisting complex data analyses, the tool has become so widely used
    there is no way the AI genie is going back in the bottle. But while
    the new technology can make research and study more efficient and
    thorough, academic integrity can easily be compromised when it is
    not properly used. Professor Paul Yip Siu-fai of the social work and
    social administration department at the University of Hong Kong has
    rightly apologised on behalf of himself and his PhD student, Bai
    Yiming, for a paper with “AI hallucinations” – a term for
    AI-generated work that is inaccurate, misleading or fabricated. At
    least 20 out of 61 references in the paper on Hong Kong’s population
    and fertility appear to be non-existent.

-   from:https://www.scmp.com/opinion/comment/article/3332441/timely-review-ai-guidelines-hong-kong-universities-essential?module=perpetual_scroll_0&pgtype=article
    ” (cheating example by 郭承恩 G8) ” New Information -Studies have
    shown significant bias in GPT (generative pre-trained transformers;
    e.g., ChatGPT) against non-native English speakers. For example, one
    study shows over half of non-native English writing samples were
    misclassified as AI generated (while the accuracy for native English
    speakers was nearly perfect). Part of the problem is that GPT
    detectors are programmed to recognize language that is more literary
    and complex as more “human.” Thus, writers not using such language
    are more likely to be categorized as using AI-generated content and
    could be unjustly flagged for AI plagiarism. Non-English-speaking
    students may be falsely accused of cheating, which can undermine
    their academic career and damage their psyches. When assessing the
    use of non-native English speakers, it might be best not to use GPT
    detectors as assessment tools until the detectors have gone through
    a more comprehensive evaluation. They can be more safely employed as
    educational aids, helping students become better writers.

-   ” (Potential Bias in AI Algorithms by 郭承恩 G8) “     New
    Information - Research studies show that one of the key drawbacks in
    academic settings is the increasing reliance on AI tools. As
    Cambra-Fierro et al. (2024) state, over-reliance on AI for
    communication, especially in recreational contexts, may reduce
    face-to-face social interactions, negatively impacting interpersonal
    skills and emotional intelligence. Students may become more isolated
    and less adept at real-world social interactions and teamwork, which
    are critical to their overall social well-being and development. -  
      ” (Negative Argumentation of Study No.1 by 吳翰威 G8) “     New
    Information - Generally, in higher education, students and academics
    may feel overwhelmed by the increasing reliance on AI tools,
    particularly when they lack sufficient training or technological
    literacy. This technostress (i.e., the anxiety and discomfort that
    individuals experience when interacting with new technologies) can
    negatively affect both mental health and academic performance -  
      ” (Negative impacts on the higher education environment by 吳翰威
    G8) “     New Information - When the usage and dependency of AI are
    increased, this will automatically limit the human brain’s thinking
    capacity. This, as a result, rapidly decreases the thinking capacity
    of humans. This removes intelligence capacities from humans and
    makes them more artificial. In addition, so much interaction with
    technology has pushed us to think like algorithms without
    understanding. Another issue is the human dependency on AI
    technology in almost every walk of life. Undoubtedly, it has
    improved living standards and made life easier, but it has impacted
    human life miserably and made humans impatient and lazy. It will
    slowly and gradually starve the human brain of thoughtfulness and
    mental efforts as it gets deep into each activity, like planning and
    organizing. High-level reliance on AI may degrade professional
    skills and generate stress when physical or brain measures are
    needed. -     ” (Negative Argumentation of Study No.2 by 吳翰威 G8)
    “     New Information - The role of AI in decision-making in
    educational institutions is spreading daily. Universities are using
    AI in both academic and administrative activities. From students
    searching for program admission requirements to the issuance of
    degrees, they are now assisted by AI personalization, tutoring,
    quick responses, 24/7 access to learning, answering questions, and
    task automation are the leading roles AI plays in the education
    sector.Still, AI technologies have severe negative consequences, and
    the limitation of their role in human decision-making is one of
    them. Slowly and gradually, AI limits and replaces the human role in
    decision-making. Human mental capabilities like intuitive analysis,
    critical thinking, and creative problem-solving are getting out of
    decision-making. Consequently, this will lead to their loss as there
    is a saying, use it or lose it.  -     ” (Use it or lose it by
    吳翰威 G8) “     My Idea - Recently, a new term has emerged to

    (reason 3 by 李其樺 G8) ” New Information - While AI promises
    efficiency and convenience, its overuse can pose serious risks to
    intellectual integrity and human creativity. Relying heavily on
    tools like ChatGPT often leads to writing that sounds generic and
    formulaic, lacking personal voice and emotional depth. This reduces
    the uniqueness and authenticity that come from individual thinking
    and experience. In addition, using AI to complete writing or
    creative tasks raises ethical concerns, including plagiarism and the
    devaluation of original human work. Many educational institutions
    and platforms have already begun restricting AI use due to issues
    such as inaccurate, biased, or inauthentic outputs. Therefore,
    instead of depending on AI to think for us, we should focus on
    developing our own critical thinking and creativity, since true
    innovation ultimately comes from human experience rather than
    algorithmic generation.
